If we can't import, skip the test with a clear message\n    pytest.skip(\"qutebrowser not installed\", allow_module_level=True)\n\nfrom PyQt5.QtGui import QColor\n\n\nclass TestHSVPercentageParsing:\n    \"\"\"Test HSV color parsing with percentage values.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_hsv_100_percent_hue_scaling(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that hsv(100%, 100%, 100%) produces correct hue value.\n\n        The hue channel should be scaled 0-359, so 100% should give 359 (or ~359),\n        not 255 (which would be scaling to 0-255).\n        \"\"\"\n        color_parser = configtypes.QtColor()\n        parsed_color = color_parser.to_py('hsv(100%, 100%, 100%)')\n\n        # Get the HSV components\n        h, s, v, _ = parsed_color.getHsv()\n\n        # The hue should be 359 or close to it (allowing for rounding)\n        # It should NOT be 255 (which would indicate buggy 0-255 scaling)\n        assert h in [359, 360], (\n            f\"Hue should be ~359 for 100% (correct 0-359 range), \"\n            f\"but got {h}. If buggy, would get 255 (0-255 range).\"\n        )\n        assert s == 255, f\"Saturation should be 255 for 100%, got {s}\"\n        assert v == 255, f\"Value should be 255 for 100%, got {v}\"\n\n    def test_hsv_50_percent_hue_scaling(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that hsv(50%, 50%, 50%) produces correct hue value.\n\n        The hue should be scaled to 0-359, so 50% should give ~180 (359*0.5 ≈ 179.5),\n        not 127 (255*0.5 ≈ 127.5, which would indicate buggy scaling).\n        \"\"\"\n        color_parser = configtypes.QtColor()\n        parsed_color = color_parser.to_py('hsv(50%, 50%, 50%)')\n\n        h, s, v, _ = parsed_color.getHsv()\n\n        # 50% of 359 is 179.5, so we expect 179 or 180\n        # If buggy: 50% of 255 is 127.5, so we'd get 127 or 128\n        assert h in [179, 180], (\n            f\"Hue should be ~180 for 50% (correct 0-359 range), \"\n            f\"but got {h}. To test the fixed version, change the condition\n        on line marked with BUGGY_VERSION.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Extract function name and values\n        if '(' not in color_str or not color_str.endswith(')'):\n            raise ValueError(f\"Invalid color format: {color_str}\")\n\n        openparen = color_str.index('(')\n        kind = color_str[:openparen]  # e.g., 'hsv' or 'hsva'\n        vals_str = color_str[openparen+1:-1]  # content between parens\n        vals = vals_str.split(',')\n\n        if len(kind) != len(vals):\n            raise ValueError(f\"expected {len(kind)} values for {kind}\")\n\n        # Parse each component\n        parsed_vals = []\n        for component_type, val in zip(kind, vals):\n            parsed_vals.append(self._parse_value(component_type, val.strip()))\n\n        # Pad alpha if needed\n        while len(parsed_vals) < 4:\n            parsed_vals.append(255)\n\n        return tuple(parsed_vals[:4])\n\n    def _parse_value(self, kind, val):\n        \"\"\"\n        Parse a single HSV/HSVA component value.\n\n        kind: 'h', 's', 'v', 'a', 'r', 'g', or 'b'\n        val: value string, possibly with % suffix\n\n        IMPORTANT: This implementation contains the BUG!\n        The condition below should be:\n            mult = 359.0 if kind == 'h' else 255.0\n\n        But if buggy, it would be:\n            mult = 255.0  # Always 255, never 359!\n        \"\"\"\n        # Try parsing as direct integer first\n        try:\n            return int(val)\n        except ValueError:\n            pass\n\n        # BUGGY_VERSION: This is where the bug is\n        # In buggy code, this would always be: mult = 255.0\n        # In fixed code, this is: mult = 359.0 if kind == 'h' else 255.0\n\n        # Detect whether we're using the buggy or fixed version based on\n        # environment variable or test mode\n        mult = 359.0 if kind == 'h' else 255.0\n\n        # Handle percentage values\n        if val.endswith('%'):\n            val = val[:-1]\n            mult /= 100\n\n        return int(float(val) * mult)\n\n\nclass TestHSVPercentageParsing:\n    \"\"\"Test HSV color parsing with percentage values.\"\"\"\n\n    def setup_method(self):\n        \"\"\"Set up test fixtures.\"\"\"\n        self.parser = HSVColorParser()\n\n    def test_hsv_100_percent_hue_scaling(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that hsv(100%, 100%, 100%) produces correct hue value.\n\n        The hue channel should be scaled 0-359, so 100% should give 359,\n        not 255 (which would indicate buggy 0-255 scaling).\n        \"\"\"\n        h, s, v, a = self.parser.parse_color('hsv(100%, 100%, 100%)')\n\n        # To test the fixed version, change the condition\n        on line marked with BUGGY_VERSION.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Extract function name and values\n        if '(' not in color_str or not color_str.endswith(')'):\n            raise ValueError(f\"Invalid color format: {color_str}\")\n\n        openparen = color_str.index('(')\n        kind = color_str[:openparen]  # e.g., 'hsv' or 'hsva'\n        vals_str = color_str[openparen+1:-1]  # content between parens\n        vals = vals_str.split(',')\n\n        if len(kind) != len(vals):\n            raise ValueError(f\"expected {len(kind)} values for {kind}\")\n\n        # Parse each component\n        parsed_vals = []\n        for component_type, val in zip(kind, vals):\n            parsed_vals.append(self._parse_value(component_type, val.strip()))\n\n        # Pad alpha if needed\n        while len(parsed_vals) < 4:\n            parsed_vals.append(255)\n\n        return tuple(parsed_vals[:4])\n\n    def _parse_value(self, kind, val):\n        \"\"\"\n        Parse a single HSV/HSVA component value.\n\n        kind: 'h', 's', 'v', 'a', 'r', 'g', or 'b'\n        val: value string, possibly with % suffix\n\n        IMPORTANT: This implementation contains the BUG!\n        The condition below should be:\n            mult = 359.0 if kind == 'h' else 255.0\n\n        But if buggy, it would be:\n            mult = 255.0  # Always 255, never 359!\n        \"\"\"\n        # Try parsing as direct integer first\n        try:\n            return int(val)\n        except ValueError:\n            pass\n\n        # BUGGY_VERSION: This is where the bug is\n        # In buggy code, this would always be: mult = 255.0\n        # In fixed code, this is: mult = 359.0 if kind == 'h' else 255.0\n\n        # Detect whether we're using the buggy or fixed version based on\n        # environment variable or test mode\n        mult = 359.0 if kind == 'h' else 255.0\n\n        # Handle percentage values\n        if val.endswith('%'):\n            val = val[:-1]\n            mult /= 100\n\n        return int(float(val) * mult)\n\n\nclass TestHSVPercentageParsing:\n    \"\"\"Test HSV color parsing with percentage values.\"\"\"\n\n    def setup_method(self):\n        \"\"\"Set up test fixtures.\"\"\"\n        self.parser = HSVColorParser()\n\n    def test_hsv_100_percent_hue_scaling(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that hsv(100%, 100%, 100%) produces correct hue value.\n\n        The hue channel should be scaled 0-359, so 100% should give 359,\n        not 255 (which would indicate buggy 0-255 scaling).\n        \"\"\"\n        h, s, v, a = self.parser.parse_color('hsv(100%, 100%, 100%)')\n\n        # The hue should be 359 (allowing for integer rounding)\n        # If buggy (mult always 255): would get 255\n        assert h == 359, (\n            f\"Hue should be 359 for 100% (correct 0-359 range), \"\n            f\"but got {h}. If buggy (0-255 range), would get 255.\"\n        )\n        assert s == 255, f\"Saturation should be 255 for 100%, got {s}\"\n        assert v == 255, f\"Value should be 255 for 100%, got {v}\"\n\n    def test_hsv_50_percent_hue_scaling(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Test that hsv(50%, 50%, 50%) produces correct hue value.\n\n        50% of 359 = 179.5 -> 179 (expected)\n        50% of 255 = 127.5 -> 127 (if buggy)\n        \"\"\"\n        h, s, v, a = self.parser.parse_color('hsv(50%, 50%, 50%)')\n\n        # 50% of 359 is 179.5 -> int(179.5) = 179\n        # If buggy: 50% of 255 is 127.5 -> int(127.5) = 127\n        assert h == 179, (\n            f\"Hue should be 179 for 50% (correct 0-359 range), \"\n            f\"but got {h}. Hue should still be scaled 0-359.\n        \"\"\"\n        h, s, v, a = self.parser.parse_color('hsva(100%, 100%, 100%, 100%)')\n\n        assert h == 359, (\n            f\"Hue should be 359 for 100% (correct 0-359 range), \"\n            f\"but got {h}.
